# Israeli settlement

For conceptions of Zionist colonization in Palestine, see [Zionism as settler colonialism](70418136.md).

For other uses, see [Israeli settlement (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_settlement_\(disambiguation\)).



[West Bank](33209.md) settlements (2025)



[East Jerusalem](343508.md) settlements (2006)



[Golan Heights](7515865.md) settlements (1992)



[Gaza Strip](12047.md) settlements (1993), dismantled since the [2005 disengagement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_Gaza)

**Israeli settlements**, also called **Israeli colonies**,[^cite_note-1][^cite_note-2][^cite_note-3][^cite_note-4] are the civilian communities built by [Israel](9282173.md) throughout the [Israeli-occupied territories](2781576.md). They are populated by Israeli citizens, almost exclusively of [Jewish identity or ethnicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Jews),[^cite_note-Haklai2015-5][^cite_note-Dumper2014-6][^cite_note-7] and have been constructed on lands that Israel has militarily occupied since the [Six-Day War](29328.md) in 1967.[^cite_note-8] The international community considers [Israeli settlements to be illegal under international law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_law_and_Israeli_settlements),[^cite_note-9][^cite_note-maj-10][^cite_note-11][^cite_note-12] but Israel disputes this.[^cite_note-ot-13][^cite_note-Harel-14][^cite_note-15][^cite_note-16] In 2024, the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) (ICJ) found in [an advisory opinion](72240449.md) that Israel's occupation was illegal and ruled that Israel had "an obligation to cease immediately all new settlement activities and to evacuate all settlers" from the occupied territories.[^cite_note-Hail-17] The population of the settlements [increased rapidly over the last 35 years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_statistics_for_Israeli_settlements_in_the_West_Bank). The expansion of settlements often involves the confiscation of Palestinian land and resources, leading to displacement of Palestinian communities and creating a source of tension and conflict. Settlements are often protected by the Israeli military and are frequently flashpoints for violence against Palestinians. Furthermore, the presence of settlements and Jewish-only bypass roads creates a [fragmented Palestinian territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_territories), seriously hindering economic development and [freedom of movement for Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_freedom_of_movement).[^cite_note-18]

As of April 2025, Israeli settlements exist in the [West Bank](59076253.md#Settlement) (including [East Jerusalem](343508.md)), which is claimed by the [Palestine Liberation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organization) (PLO) as the sovereign territory of the [State of Palestine](241405.md), and in the [Golan Heights](7515865.md#Israeli_settlements), which is internationally recognized as a part of the sovereign territory of [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-21] Through the [Jerusalem Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Law) and the [Golan Heights Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Heights_Law), Israel effectively annexed both territories, though the international community has rejected any change to their [status as occupied territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Status_of_territories_occupied_by_Israel_in_1967). Although Israel's West Bank settlements have been built on territory administered under military rule rather than civil law, [Israeli civil law is "pipelined" into the settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_law_in_the_West_Bank_settlements), such that Israeli citizens living there are treated similarly to those living in Israel. Many consider it to be a major obstacle to the [Israeli–Palestinian peace process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_peace_process).[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] In *[Legal Consequences of the Construction of a Wall in the Occupied Palestinian Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_Consequences_of_the_Construction_of_a_Wall_in_the_Occupied_Palestinian_Territory)* (2004), the ICJ found that Israel's settlements and the then-nascent [Israeli West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier) were both in violation of international law; part of the latter has been constructed within the West Bank, as opposed to being entirely on Israel's side of the [Green Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Israel\)).[^cite_note-27][^cite_note-fco.gov.uk-28][^cite_note-29]

As of January 2023, there are [144 Israeli settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_settlements) in the West Bank, including 12 in East Jerusalem; the Israeli government administers the West Bank as the [Judea and Samaria Area](397650.md), which does not include East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-jl-30] In addition to the settlements, the West Bank is also hosting at least 196 [Israeli outposts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_outpost),[^cite_note-bbceye-31] which are settlements that have not been authorized by the Israeli government. In total, over 450,000 Israeli settlers reside in the West Bank, excluding East Jerusalem, with an additional 220,000 Israeli settlers residing in East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-gr-32][^cite_note-33] Additionally, over 25,000 Israeli settlers live in Syria's Golan Heights.[^cite_note-Israelpopulations-34] Between 1967 and 1982, there were 18 settlements established in the [Israeli-occupied Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_the_Sinai_Peninsula) of [Egypt](8087628.md), though these were dismantled by Israel after the [Egypt–Israel peace treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Israel_peace_treaty) of 1979. Additionally, as part of the [Israeli disengagement from the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_Gaza) in 2005, Israel dismantled all 21 settlements in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) and four settlements in the West Bank.[^cite_note-West_Bank-35]

Per the [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention), the transfer by an occupying power of its civilian population into the territory it is occupying constitutes a [war crime](48626.md),[^cite_note-36][^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38] although Israel disputes that this statute applies to the West Bank.[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] On 20 December 2019, the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) announced the opening of [an investigation of war crimes in the Palestinian territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Palestine). The presence and ongoing expansion of existing settlements by Israel and the construction of outposts is frequently criticized as an obstacle to peace by the PLO,[^cite_note-41] and by a number of third parties, such as the [Organization of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md),[^cite_note-42] the [United Nations](31769.md) (UN),[^cite_note-BBCObstacle2-43] Russia,[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45] the United Kingdom,[^cite_note-46] France,[^cite_note-47] and the [European Union](9317.md).[^cite_note-48] The UN has repeatedly upheld the view that Israel's construction of settlements in the occupied territories constitutes a violation of the Fourth Geneva Convention.[^cite_note-UN_Resolutions_446,_452,_and_4652-49][^cite_note-50][^cite_note-ic2-51] For decades, the United States also designated Israeli settlements as illegal,[^cite_note-BBCObstacle2-43] but the [first Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Trump_administration) reversed this long-standing policy in November 2019,[^cite_note-52] declaring that "the establishment of Israeli civilian settlements in the West Bank is not *[per se](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/per%20se)* inconsistent with international law";[^cite_note-53] this new policy, in turn, was reversed to the original by the [Biden administration](65732174.md) in February 2024, once again classifying Israeli settlement expansion as "inconsistent with international law" and matching the official positions of the other three members of the [Middle East Quartet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quartet_on_the_Middle_East).[^cite_note-54][^cite_note-55]

## Name and characterization

Certain observers and Palestinians occasionally use the term "Israeli colonies" as a substitute for the term "settlements".[^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58][^cite_note-59] Settlements range in character from farming communities and frontier villages to urban suburbs and neighborhoods. The four largest settlements, [Modi'in Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modi'in_Illit), [Ma'ale Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ale_Adumim), [Beitar Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beitar_Illit) and [Ariel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_\(city\)), have achieved city status. Ariel has 18,000 residents, while the rest have around 37,000 to 55,500 each.

## Housing costs and state subventions

Settlement has an economic dimension, much of it driven by the significantly lower costs of housing for Israeli citizens living in Israeli settlements compared to the cost of housing and living in Israel proper.[^cite_note-maannews.net-60] Government spending per citizen in the settlements is double that spent per Israeli citizen in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and [Jerusalem](16043.md), while government spending for settlers in isolated Israeli settlements is three times the Israeli national average. Most of the spending goes to the security of the Israeli citizens living there.[^cite_note-Rudoren-61]

## Number of settlements and inhabitants

Main article: [List of Israeli settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_settlements)

As of January 2023, there are 144 Israeli settlements in the [West Bank](33209.md), including 12 in [East Jerusalem](343508.md).[^cite_note-jl-30] In addition, there are at least 196 [Israeli illegal outposts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_outpost) (not sanctioned by the Israeli government) in the West Bank.[^cite_note-bbceye-31] In total, over 529,000 Israeli settlers live in the West Bank excluding East Jerusalem, with an additional 246,000 Jewish settlers residing in East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-62][^cite_note-63][^cite_note-gr-32][^cite_note-64]

Additionally, over 20,000 Israeli citizens live in settlements in the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-Sherwood-66]

## History

See also: [Israeli settlement timeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_settlement_timeline)

### Occupied territories

Following the 1967 [Six-Day War](29328.md), Israel [occupied](816214.md) a number of territories.[^cite_note-fmep1-67] It took over the remainder of the Palestinian Mandate territories of the [West Bank including East Jerusalem](59076253.md), from [Jordan](7515964.md) which had [controlled the territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_annexation_of_the_West_Bank) since the 1948 Arab-Israeli war, and the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) from [Egypt](8087628.md), which had [held Gaza under occupation since 1949](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_of_the_Gaza_Strip_by_Egypt). From Egypt, it also captured the [Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula) and from [Syria](7515849.md) it captured most of the [Golan Heights](7515865.md), which [since 1981 has been administered under the Golan Heights Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Status_of_the_Golan_Heights).

### Settlement policy

As early as September 1967, Israeli settlement policy was progressively encouraged by the [Labor government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteenth_government_of_Israel) of [Levi Eshkol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi_Eshkol). The basis for Israeli settlement in the West Bank became the [Allon Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allon_Plan),[^cite_note-HRW_Separate-IV-68][^cite_note-69] named after its inventor [Yigal Allon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yigal_Allon). It implied Israeli annexation of major parts of the [Israeli-occupied territories](2781576.md), especially [East Jerusalem](343508.md), [Gush Etzion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Etzion) and the [Jordan Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Valley_\(Middle_East\)).[^cite_note-70] The settlement policy of the government of Yitzhak Rabin was also derived from the Allon Plan.[^cite_note-Knesset-71]

The first settlement was [Kfar Etzion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Etzion), in the southern West Bank,[^cite_note-HRW_Separate-IV-68][^cite_note-72] although that location was outside the Allon Plan. Many settlements began as [Nahal settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahal_settlement). They were established as military outposts and later expanded and populated with civilian inhabitants. According to a secret document dating to 1970, obtained by [Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz), the settlement of [Kiryat Arba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Arba) was established by confiscating land by military order and falsely representing the project as being strictly for military use while in reality, Kiryat Arba was planned for settler use. The method of confiscating land by military order for establishing civilian settlements was an open secret in Israel throughout the 1970s, but publication of the information was suppressed by the [military censor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Military_Censor).[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74]

In the 1970s, Israel's methods for seizing Palestinian land to establish settlements included requisitioning for ostensibly military purposes and spraying of land with poison.[^cite_note-75]

The Likud government of Menahem Begin, from 1977, was more supportive to settlement in other parts of the West Bank, by organizations like [Gush Emunim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Emunim) and the [Jewish Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Agency_for_Israel)/World Zionist Organization, and intensified the settlement activities.[^cite_note-Knesset-71][^cite_note-DPR-76][^cite_note-Lustick-77] In a government statement, Likud declared that the entire historic Land of Israel is the inalienable heritage of the Jewish people and that no part of the West Bank should be handed over to foreign rule.[^cite_note-78] Ariel Sharon declared in the same year (1977) that there was a plan to settle 2 million Jews in the West Bank by 2000.[^cite_note-79] The government abrogated the prohibition from purchasing occupied land by Israelis; the "Drobles Plan", a plan for large-scale settlement in the West Bank meant to prevent a Palestinian state under the pretext of security became the framework for its policy.[^cite_note-Drobles-80][^cite_note-81] The "Drobles Plan" from the [World Zionist Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Zionist_Organization), dated October 1978 and named "Master Plan for the Development of Settlements in Judea and Samaria, 1979–1983", was written by the Jewish Agency director and former Knesset member [Matityahu Drobles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matityahu_Drobles). In January 1981, the government adopted a follow-up plan from Drobles, dated September 1980 and named "The current state of the settlements in Judea and Samaria", with more details about settlement strategy and policy.[^cite_note-Drobles2-82][^cite_note-83]



Israeli soldiers searching a Palestinian in [Tel Rumeida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Rumeida), 2012

Since 1967, government-funded settlement projects in the West Bank are implemented by the "Settlement Division" of the [World Zionist Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Zionist_Organization).[^cite_note-JP_Cabinet_seeks_limit-84] Though formally a [non-governmental organization](46539.md), it is funded by the Israeli government and leases lands from the Civil Administration to settle in the West Bank. It is authorized to create settlements in the West Bank on lands licensed to it by the Civil Administration.[^cite_note-HRW_Separate-IV-68] Traditionally, the Settlement Division has been under the responsibility of the Agriculture Ministry. Since the Oslo Accords, it was always housed within the Prime Minister's Office (PMO). In 2007, it was moved back to the Agriculture Ministry. In 2009, the Netanyahu Government decided to subject all settlement activities to additional approval of the Prime Minister and the Defense Minister. In 2011, Netanyahu sought to move the Settlement Division again under the direct control of (his own) PMO, and to curtail Defense Minister Ehud Barak's authority.[^cite_note-JP_Cabinet_seeks_limit-84]

At the presentation of the [Oslo II Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_II_Accord) on 5 October 1995 in the Knesset, PM [Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin) expounded the Israeli settlement policy in connection with the permanent solution to the conflict. Israel wanted *"a Palestinian entity, less than a state, which will be a home to most of the Palestinian residents living in the Gaza Strip and the West Bank"*. It wanted to keep settlements beyond the [Green Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Israel\)) including Ma'ale Adumim and Givat Ze'ev in East Jerusalem. Blocs of settlements should be established in the West Bank. Rabin promised not to return to the 4 June 1967 lines.[^cite_note-85]

In June 1997, the Likud government of [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) presented its "Allon Plus Plan". This plan holds the retention of some 60% of the West Bank, including the "Greater Jerusalem" area with the settlements Gush Etzion and Ma'aleh Adumim, other large concentrations of settlements in the West Bank, the entire Jordan Valley, a "security area", and a network of Israeli-only bypass roads.[^cite_note-CEIRPP_V-86][^cite_note-FMEP_7_4-87]



Israeli settlers in the [Ofra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ofra) settlement, [Israeli-occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupied_territories) [West Bank](33209.md), 2012

In the [Road map for peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_map_for_peace) of 2002, which was never implemented, the establishment of a Palestinian state was acknowledged. Outposts would be dismantled. However, many new outposts appeared instead, few were removed. Israel's settlement policy remained unchanged. Settlements in East Jerusalem and remaining West Bank were expanded.

While according to official Israeli policy no new settlements were built, at least some hundred [unauthorized outposts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_outpost) were established since 2002 with [state funding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasson_Report) in the 60% of the West Bank that was not under [Palestinian administrative control](24093.md) and the population growth of settlers did not diminish.

In 2005, all 21 settlements in the Gaza Strip and four in the northern West Bank were forcibly evacuated as part of [Israeli disengagement from the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_the_Gaza_Strip), known to some in Israel as "the Expulsion".[^cite_note-West_Bank-35] Nevertheless, the total settler population continued to rise.[^cite_note-fmep1972-2010-88]

After the failure of the Roadmap, several new plans emerged to settle in major parts of the West Bank. In 2011, *Haaretz* revealed the [Civil Administration's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Civil_Administration) *"Blue Line"*-plan, written in January 2011, which aims to increase Israeli "state-ownership" of [West Bank land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declarations_of_State_Land_in_the_West_Bank) ("state lands") and settlement in strategic areas like the Jordan Valley and the northern Dead Sea area.[^cite_note-Eldar_220711-89] In March 2012, it was revealed that the Civil Administration over the years covertly allotted 10% of the West Bank for further settlement. Provisional names for future new settlements or settlement expansions were already assigned. The plan includes many Palestinian built-up sites in the [Areas A and B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Oslo_Accords).[^cite_note-Eldar_300312-90]

### Settlements in the Gaza Strip

See also: [Proposed Israeli resettlement of the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_Israeli_resettlement_of_the_Gaza_Strip)



Settlement area in the Gaza Strip (March 1999)

Land in the Gaza Strip available to its Palestinian inhabitants has historically been limited as a result of Israeli land confiscation and the establishment of settlements. Settlement growth in the Gaza Strip before 1977 was limited, as the Israeli labor party's policy of containment preferred the establishment of a collection of settlements along the border of the Strip. At this point, 6 settlements in the Strip existed, Kfar Darom, Netzarim, Morag, Eretz, Katif, and Netzer Hazani. With the Likud party's revisionist Zionist policies entering with Begin's government, the scale of settlement expansion increased, although the basic policies relating to the settlements did not change. By 1978, 13 settlements had been built as part of a buffer zone along Gaza's southern border in Rafah.[^cite_note-Sara_M._Roy2-91]

The discussions at [Camp David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_David_Accords) that year surrounding the idea of potential future Palestinian autonomy would trigger an increase in settlement expansion in the Gaza Strip, following the Israeli policy of establishing "[facts on the ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Facts_on_the_ground)". Political economist Sara Roy described this as a policy intended to make the establishment of an independent Palestinian state more difficult. The locations and size of these new settlements would contribute to geographically isolating Palestinian communities from each other.[^cite_note-Sara_M._Roy2-91]

In the seven years between 1978 and 1985, 11,500 acres of land were confiscated by the Israeli government for the establishment of settlements. By 1991, the settler population in Gaza would reach 3,500 and 4,000 by 1993, or less than 1% of Gaza's population. The land available for use by the Jewish settler community exceeded 25% of the total land in Gaza. The ratio of dunams to people was 23 for Jewish settlers, and 0.27 for Palestinians. Comparing the available built-up area available to each of the two groups in 1993, the ratio is 115 people per square mile for Jewish settlers and over 9,000 people per square mile for Palestinians. Sara Roy estimates the increase in Palestinian population density in Gaza due to Israeli policies alone to be an increase of almost 2,000 people per square mile in 1993.[^cite_note-Sara_M._Roy2-91]

All the settlements were surrounded by electric fences or barbed wire.[^cite_note-92]

While the settlements maintained an isolated economic system, they affected the Gazan economy via land confiscation, the disproportionate consumption of local resources such as water, by overwhelmingly denying work opportunities and through the large disparities in funding (both private and governmental) for economic development.[^cite_note-Sara_M._Roy2-91]

Some settlements are self-contained cities with a stable population in the tens of thousands, infrastructure, and all other features of permanence. Examples are [Beitar Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beitar_Illit) (a city of close to 45,000 residents), [Ma'ale Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ale_Adumim), [Modi'in Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modi'in_Illit), and [Ariel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_\(city\)) (almost 20,000 residents). Some are towns with a [local council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_council_\(Israel\)) status with populations of 2,000–20,0000, such as [Alfei Menashe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfei_Menashe), [Eli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli,_Mateh_Binyamin), [Elkana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elkana), [Efrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efrat_\(Israeli_settlement\)) and [Kiryat Arba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Arba). There are also clusters of villages governed by a local elected committee and [regional councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_council_\(Israel\)) that are responsible for municipal services. Examples are [Kfar Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Adumim), [Neve Daniel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neve_Daniel), [Kfar Tapuach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Tapuach) and [Ateret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ateret). [Kibbutzim](106200.md) and [moshavim](1597952.md) in the territories include [Argaman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argaman), [Gilgal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilgal,_Bik'at_HaYarden), [Na'aran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Na'aran_\(Israeli_settlement\)) and [Yitav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitav). Jewish neighborhoods have been built on the outskirts of Arab neighborhoods, for example in [Hebron](38577.md). In Jerusalem, there are urban neighborhoods where Jews and Arabs live together: the [Muslim Quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Quarter_\(Jerusalem\)), [Silwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silwan), [Abu Tor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Tor), [Sheikh Jarrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Jarrah) and [Shimon HaTzadik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_HaTzadik).

Under the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords), the West Bank was divided into three separate parts designated as [Area A, Area B and Area C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Oslo_Accords). Leaving aside the position of East Jerusalem, all of the settlements are in [Area C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_C_\(West_Bank\)) which comprises about 60% of the West Bank.

## Types of settlement

- Cities/towns: [Ariel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_\(city\)), [Betar Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betar_Illit), [Modi'in Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modi'in_Illit) and [Ma'ale Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ale_Adumim).

- [Urban suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburb), such as [Har Gilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Har_Gilo).

- [Block settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Block_settlement), such as [Gush Etzion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Etzion) and settlements in the [Nablus](233369.md) area.

- Frontier villages, such as those along the Jordan River.

- [Outposts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_outpost), small settlements, some authorized and some unauthorized, often on hilltops. The [Sasson Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasson_Report), commissioned by [Ariel Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_Sharon)'s administration, found that several government ministries had cooperated to establish illegal outposts, spending millions of dollars on infrastructure.

  [^cite_note-93]

## Resettlement of former Jewish communities

Some settlements were established on sites where Jewish communities had existed during the [British Mandate of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine) or even since the [First Aliyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Aliyah) or ancient times.[^cite_note-94]

- [Golan Heights](7515865.md) – [Bnei Yehuda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bnei_Yehuda,_Golan_Heights), founded in 1890,

  [^cite_note-95]

  &#x20;abandoned because of Arab attacks in 1920, rebuilt near the original site in 1972.

- [Jerusalem](16043.md) – Jewish presence alongside other peoples since biblical times, various surrounding communities and neighborhoods, including Kfar Shiloah, also known as [Silwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silwan)—settled by Yemenite Jews in 1884, Jewish residents evacuated in 1938, a few Jewish families move into reclaimed homes in 2004.

  [^cite_note-96]

  &#x20;Other communities: [Shimon HaTzadik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_HaTzadik), [Neve Yaakov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neve_Yaakov) and [Atarot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atarot) which in post-1967 was rebuilt as an industrial zone.

- [Gush Etzion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Etzion) – four communities, established between 1927 and 1947, destroyed 1948, reestablished beginning 1967.

  [^cite_note-97]

- [Hebron](38577.md) – Jewish presence since biblical times, forced out in the wake of the [1929 Hebron massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1929_Hebron_massacre), some families returned in 1931 but were evacuated by the British, a few buildings resettled since 1967.

  [^cite_note-98]

- [Dead Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea), northern area – [Kalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalia,_West_Bank) and [Beit HaArava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_HaArava) – the former was built in 1934 as a [kibbutz](106200.md) for [potash](56509.md) mining. The latter was built in 1943 as an agricultural community. Both were abandoned in 1948, and subsequently destroyed by [Jordanian forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Legion),

  [^cite_note-99]

  &#x20;and resettled after the Six-Day War.

- [Gaza City](55387.md) had a Jewish community for many centuries that was evacuated following riots in 1929.

  [^cite_note-100]

  &#x20;After the Six-Day War, Jewish communities were not built in Gaza City, but in [Gush Katif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Katif) in the southwestern part of the Gaza Strip, f.e. [Kfar Darom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Darom) – established in 1946, evacuated in 1948 after an Egyptian attack,

  [^cite_note-101]

  &#x20;resettled in 1970, evacuated in 2005 as part of the [withdrawal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel's_unilateral_disengagement_plan) from the [Gaza Strip](12047.md).

  [^cite_note-102]

## Demographics

See also: [List of Israeli settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_settlements) and [Population statistics for Israeli West Bank settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_statistics_for_Israeli_West_Bank_settlements)



Settler population by year in the [West Bank](33209.md), [Gaza Strip](12047.md), [East Jerusalem](343508.md) and [Golan Heights](7515865.md) 1972–2007

[^cite_note-FmepByYear-103]

[^cite_note-BtselemByYear-104]

At the end of 2010, 534,224 Jewish Israelis lived in the West Bank, including East Jerusalem. 314,132 of them lived in the 121 authorised settlements and 102 unauthorised settlement outposts on the West Bank, 198,629 were living in East Jerusalem, and almost 20,000 lived in settlements in the Golan Heights.

By 2011, the number of Jewish settlers in the West Bank excluding East Jerusalem had increased to 328,423 people.[^cite_note-fmep1972-2010-88]

In June 2014, the number of Israeli settlers in the West Bank excluding East Jerusalem had increased to 382,031 people, with over 20,000 Israeli settlers in the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-105][^cite_note-Sherwood-66]

In January 2015, the Israeli Interior Ministry gave figures of 389,250 Israeli citizens living in the West Bank outside East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-106]

By the end of 2016, the West Bank Jewish population had risen to 420,899, excluding East Jerusalem, where there were more than 200,000 Jews.[^cite_note-107]

In 2019, the number of Israeli settlers in the West Bank excluding East Jerusalem had risen to 441,600 individuals,[^cite_note-gr-32] and the number of Israeli settlers in the Golan Heights had risen to 25,261.[^cite_note-Israelpopulations-34]

In 2020, the number of Israeli settlers in the West Bank excluding East Jerusalem had reportedly risen to 451,700 individuals, with an additional 220,000 Jews living in East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-gr-32]

Based on various sources,[^cite_note-fmep1972-2010-88][^cite_note-Jerusalem_Post-16_May_2014-108][^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110][^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113][^cite_note-Middle_East_Eye-2_September_2014-114] population dispersal can be estimated as follows:

| Gaza Strip <sup>2</sup> | 30 (see [Kfar Darom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Darom)) | 700 <sup>1</sup> | – | 900 | 4,800 | 7,826 | 0 |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |

&#x20;including Sinai

&#x20;Janet Abu-Lughod mentions 500 settlers in Gaza in 1978 (excluding Sinai), and 1,000 in 1980

[^cite_note-conquest_colony-124]

In addition to internal migration, in large though declining numbers, the settlements absorb annually about 1000 new immigrants from outside Israel. The American Kulanu organization works with such right-wing Israeli settler groups as [Amishav and Shavei Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shavei_Israel) to settle "lost" Jews of color in such areas where local Palestinians are being displaced.[^cite_note-125] In the 1990s, the annual settler population growth was more than three times the annual population growth in Israel.[^cite_note-126] Population growth has continued in the 2000s.[^cite_note-127] According to the BBC, the settlements in the West Bank have been growing at a rate of 5–6% since 2001.[^cite_note-bbc-25November2009-128] In 2016, there were sixty thousand [American Israelis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_diaspora) living in settlements in the West Bank.[^cite_note-129]

The establishment of settlements in the Palestinian territories is linked to the displacement of the Palestinian populations as evidenced by a 1979 Security Council Commission which established a link between Israeli settlements and the displacement of the local population. The commission also found that those who remained were under consistent pressure to leave to make room for further settlers who were being encouraged into the area. In conclusion the commission stated that settlement in the Palestinian territories was causing "profound and irreversible changes of a geographic and demographic nature".[^cite_note-Jacques2012-130]

## Administration and local government

### West Bank

Main article: [Judea and Samaria Area](397650.md)



Map of the [West Bank](33209.md) and the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) with Israeli Settlements, 2007



[Yamit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yamit) in the [Sinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula), between 1975 and 1980, evacuated by Israel in 1982



[Neve Dekalim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neve_Dekalim), Gaza Strip, [evacuated by Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel's_unilateral_disengagement_plan) in 2005

The Israeli settlements in the West Bank fall under the administrative district of *[Judea and Samaria Area](397650.md)*. Since December 2007, approval by both the Israeli Prime Minister and Israeli Defense Minister of all settlement activities (including planning) in the West Bank is required.[^cite_note-131] Authority for planning and construction is held by the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) [Civil Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Civil_Administration).

The area consists of four [cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_council_\(Israel\)), thirteen [local councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_council_\(Israel\)) and six [regional councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_council_\(Israel\)).

- Cities: [Ariel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_\(city\)), [Betar Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betar_Illit), [Maale Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maale_Adumim), [Modi'in Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modi'in_Illit);

- Local councils: [Alfei Menashe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfei_Menashe), [Beit Aryeh-Ofarim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Aryeh-Ofarim), [Beit El](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_El), [Efrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efrat_\(Israeli_settlement\)), [Elkana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elkana), [Giv'at Ze'ev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giv'at_Ze'ev), [Har Adar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Har_Adar), [Immanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immanuel_\(town\)), [Karnei Shomron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karnei_Shomron), [Kedumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kedumim), [Kiryat Arba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Arba), [Ma'ale Efraim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ale_Efraim), [Oranit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oranit);

- Regional councils: [Gush Etzion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Etzion) (*Ezion Bloc*), [Har Hebron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Har_Hebron_Regional_Council) (*[Mount Hebron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Hebron)*), [Matte Binyamin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mateh_Binyamin_Regional_Council) (*Staff of [Benjamin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribe_of_Benjamin)*, named after the ancient [Israelite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israelites) tribe that dwelled in the area), [Megilot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megilot_Regional_Council) (*Scrolls*, named after the [Dead Sea scrolls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea_scrolls), which were discovered in the area), [Shomron Regional Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shomron_Regional_Council) (*[Samaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samaria)*), [Biq'at HaYarden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bik'at_HaYarden_Regional_Council) (*[Jordan valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Valley_\(Middle_East\))*).

The [Yesha Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yesha_Council) ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): מועצת יש"ע, *Moatzat Yesha*, a Hebrew acronym for [Judea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judea), [Samaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samaria) and [Gaza](12047.md)) is the umbrella organization of municipal councils in the West Bank.

The actual buildings of the Israeli settlements cover only one percent of the West Bank, but their jurisdiction and their regional councils extend to about 42 percent of the West Bank, according to the Israeli NGO [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem). Yesha Council chairman [Dani Dayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dani_Dayan) disputes the figures and claims that the settlements only control 9.2 percent of the West Bank.[^cite_note-CBSmain6650897-132]

Between 2001 and 2007 more than 10,000 Israeli settlement units were built, while 91 permits were issued for Palestinian construction, and 1,663 Palestinian structures were demolished in Area C.[^cite_note-133]

West Bank Palestinians have their cases tried in Israel's military courts while Jewish Israeli settlers living in the same occupied territory are tried in civil courts.[^cite_note-134] The arrangement has been described as "de facto segregation" by the UN Committee on the Elimination of Racial Discrimination.[^cite_note-135] A bill to formally extend Israeli law to the Israeli settlements in the West Bank was rejected in 2012.[^cite_note-136] The basic military laws governing the West Bank are influenced by what is called the "pipelining" of Israeli legislation. As a result of "[enclave law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enclave_law)", large portions of Israeli [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)) are applied to Israeli settlements and Israeli residents in the occupied territories.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBen-NaftaliSfardViterbo201852-137]

On 31 August 2014, Israel announced it was appropriating 400 hectares of land in the West Bank to eventually house 1,000 Israel families. The appropriation was described as the largest in more than 30 years.[^cite_note-NewSettlement-138] According to reports on Israel Radio, the development is a response to the [2014 kidnapping and murder of Israeli teenagers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_kidnapping_and_murder_of_Israeli_teenagers).[^cite_note-NewSettlement-138]

In March 2024 and during the [Gaza war](74998519.md), it was announced that Israel was planning on building more than 3,300 new homes in the [Kedar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kedar_\(Israeli_settlement\)) and [Ma'ale Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ale_Adumim) settlements in the West Bank. The settlement expansion was announced by Israeli Finance Minister [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md) after three Palestinians opened fire near Ma'ale Adumim, killing one and wounding five, and drew criticism from the US due to increasing tensions.[^cite_note-139] During the Gaza war, the lines between settlers and the military were described as having become "indistinguishable".[^cite_note-140]

### East Jerusalem

East Jerusalem is defined in the [Jerusalem Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Law) of 1980 as part of Israel and its capital, [Jerusalem](16043.md). As such it is administered as part of the city and its district, the [Jerusalem District](844333.md). Pre-1967 residents of East Jerusalem and their descendants have residency status in the city but many have refused Israeli citizenship. Thus, the Israeli government maintains an administrative distinction between Israeli citizens and non-citizens in East Jerusalem, but the [Jerusalem municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kikar_Safra) does not.

### Golan Heights

The Golan Heights is administered under Israeli civil law as the Golan sub-district, a part of the [Northern District](324281.md). Israel makes no legal or administrative distinction between pre-1967 communities in the Golan Heights (mainly [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze)) and the post-1967 settlements.

### Sinai Peninsula

See also: [Israeli occupation of the Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_the_Sinai_Peninsula) and [Category\:Former Israeli settlements in Sinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Former_Israeli_settlements_in_Sinai)

After the capture of the Sinai Peninsula from [Egypt](8087628.md) in the 1967 Six-Day War, settlements were established along the Gulf of Aqaba and in northeast Sinai, just below the [Gaza Strip](12047.md). Israel had plans to expand the settlement of [Yamit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yamit) into a city with a population of 200,000,[^cite_note-141] though the actual population of Yamit did not exceed 3,000.[^cite_note-142] The Sinai Peninsula was returned to Egypt in stages beginning in 1979 as part of the [Egypt–Israel peace treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Israel_peace_treaty). As required by the treaty, in 1982 Israel evacuated the Israeli civilian population from the 18 Sinai settlements in Sinai. In some instances evacuations were done forcefully, such as the evacuation of Yamit. All the settlements were then dismantled.

### Gaza Strip

See also: [Population statistics for Israeli Gaza Strip settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_statistics_for_Israeli_Gaza_Strip_settlements)

Before [Israel's unilateral disengagement plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel's_unilateral_disengagement_plan) in which the Israeli settlements were evacuated, there were [21 settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Former_Israeli_settlements_in_the_Gaza_Strip) in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) under the administration of the [Hof Aza Regional Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hof_Aza_Regional_Council). The land was allocated in such a way that each Israeli settler disposed of 400 times the land available to the Palestinian refugees, and 20 times the volume of water allowed to the peasant farmers of the Strip.[^cite_note-143]

## Legal status

Main article: [International law and Israeli settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_law_and_Israeli_settlements)



[Gilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilo), East Jerusalem



[Pisgat Ze'ev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pisgat_Ze'ev), East Jerusalem



[Katzrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katzrin), Golan Heights

The [International Court of Justice](14918.md) delivered a landmark advisory opinion in July 2024 that Israel's occupation of West Bank, East Jerusalem and the Gaza Strip was illegal, that Israel had "an obligation to cease immediately all new settlement activities and to evacuate all settlers" from the West Bank and East Jerusalem, and that Israel should "make reparation for the damage caused to all" the people of such lands.[^cite_note-Hail-17][^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145]

The consensus view[^cite_note-146] in the [international community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_community) is that the existence of Israeli settlements in the [West Bank](33209.md) including [East Jerusalem](343508.md) and the [Golan Heights](7515865.md) is in violation of international law.[^cite_note-bbc-2009-12-09-147] The [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention) includes statements such as "the Occupying Power shall not deport or transfer parts of its own civilian population into the territory it occupies".[^cite_note-148] On 20 December 2019, [International Criminal Court](14880.md) chief prosecutor [Fatou Bensouda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatou_Bensouda) announced an [International Criminal Court investigation in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Palestine) into alleged war crimes committed during the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md).[^cite_note-ToI-149] At present, the view of the [international community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_community), as reflected in numerous UN resolutions, regards the building and existence of Israeli settlements in the [West Bank](33209.md), [East Jerusalem](343508.md) and the [Golan Heights](7515865.md) as a violation of international law.[^cite_note-Playfair1992-150][^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152] [UN Security Council Resolution 446](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council_Resolution_446) refers to the [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention) as the applicable international legal instrument, and calls upon Israel to desist from transferring its own population into the territories or changing their demographic makeup. The reconvened Conference of the High Contracting Parties to the Geneva Conventions has declared the settlements illegal[^cite_note-153] as has the primary judicial organ of the UN, the [International Court of Justice](14918.md).[^cite_note-icj-cij.org-154]

The position of successive Israeli governments is that all authorized settlements are entirely legal and consistent with international law.[^cite_note-Mahler2004-155] In practice, Israel does not accept that the Fourth Geneva Convention applies *[de jure](43594.md)*, but has stated that on humanitarian issues it will govern itself *de facto* by its provisions, without specifying which these are.[^cite_note-Gerson,_Allan_1978,_p._82-156][^cite_note-Roberts,_Adam_1988_pp._345-359-157] The scholar and jurist [Eugene Rostow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_V._Rostow)[^cite_note-158] has disputed the illegality of authorized settlements.

Under Israeli law, West Bank settlements must meet specific criteria to be legal.[^cite_note-Ofran,_Etkes-159] In 2009, there were approximately 100[^cite_note-bbc-25November2009-128] small communities that did not meet these criteria and are referred to as [illegal outposts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_outpost#Illegal_outposts).[^cite_note-is-160][^cite_note-fmep.org-161][^cite_note-162]

In 2014 twelve EU countries warned businesses against involving themselves in the settlements. According to the warnings, economic activities relating to the settlements involve legal and economic risks stemming from the fact that the settlements are built on occupied land not recognized as Israel's.[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164]

### Illegality arguments

The consensus of the international community – the vast majority of states, the overwhelming majority of legal experts, the International Court of Justice and the UN – is that settlements are in violation of international law.[^cite_note-Waxman-165][^cite_note-ic-166] After the Six-Day War, in 1967, [Theodor Meron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodor_Meron), legal counsel to the [Israeli Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Foreign_Ministry) stated in a legal opinion to the Prime Minister,

> "My conclusion is that civilian settlement in the administered territories contravenes the explicit provisions of the Fourth Geneva Convention."[^cite_note-167]

This legal opinion was sent to Prime Minister [Levi Eshkol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi_Eshkol). However, it was not made public at the time. The Labor cabinet allowed settlements despite the warning. This paved the way for future settlement growth. In 2007, Meron stated that "I believe that I would have given the same opinion today."[^cite_note-168]

In 1978, the [Legal Adviser of the Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_Adviser_of_the_Department_of_State) of the United States reached the same conclusion.[^cite_note-is-160][^cite_note-169]

The International Court of Justice, in its advisory opinion, has since ruled that Israel is in breach of international law by establishing settlements in Occupied Palestinian Territory, including East Jerusalem. The Court maintains that Israel cannot rely on its right of self-defense or necessity to impose a regime that violates international law. The Court also ruled that Israel violates basic human rights by impeding liberty of movement and the inhabitants' right to work, health, education and an adequate standard of living.[^cite_note-170]

International intergovernmental organizations such as the Conference of the High Contracting Parties to the [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention),[^cite_note-171] major organs of the [United Nations](31769.md),[^cite_note-172] the [European Union](9317.md), and [Canada](5042916.md),[^cite_note-173] also regard the settlements as a violation of international law. The [Committee on the Elimination of Racial Discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_on_the_Elimination_of_All_Forms_of_Racial_Discrimination#Committee_on_the_Elimination_of_Racial_Discrimination) wrote that "The status of the settlements was clearly inconsistent with Article 3 of the Convention, which, as noted in the Committee's General Recommendation XIX, prohibited all forms of racial segregation in all countries. There is a consensus among publicists that the prohibition of racial discrimination, irrespective of territories, is an imperative norm of international law."[^cite_note-174] [Amnesty International](18947898.md), and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) have also characterized the settlements as a violation of international law.

In late January 2013 a report drafted by three justices, presided over by [Christine Chanet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_Chanet), and issued by the [United Nations Human Rights Council](635790.md) declared that Jewish settlements constituted a creeping annexation based on multiple violations of the [Geneva Conventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Conventions) and international law, and stated that if [Palestine](241405.md) ratified the [Rome Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_parties_to_the_Rome_Statute_of_the_International_Criminal_Court), Israel could be tried for "gross violations of human rights law and serious violations of [international humanitarian law](1093604.md)." A spokesman for Israel's [Foreign Ministry](18133296.md) declared the report 'unfortunate' and accused the UN's [Human Rights Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Council) of a "systematically one-sided and biased approach towards Israel."[^cite_note-175]

The [Supreme Court of Israel](1666510.md), with a variety of different justices sitting, has repeatedly stated that Israel's presence in the West Bank is subject to international law.[^cite_note-176]

### Legality arguments

Four prominent jurists cited the concept of the "sovereignty vacuum" in the immediate aftermath of the Six-Day War to describe the legal status of the West Bank and Gaza:[^cite_note-177] [Yehuda Zvi Blum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yehuda_Zvi_Blum) in 1968,[^cite_note-178] [Elihu Lauterpacht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elihu_Lauterpacht) in 1968,[^cite_note-179] [Julius Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Stone) in 1969[^cite_note-180] and 1981,[^cite_note-181] and [Stephen M. Schwebel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_M._Schwebel) in 1970.[^cite_note-182] [Eugene V. Rostow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_V._Rostow) also argued in 1979 that the occupied territories' legal status was undetermined.[^cite_note-183]

- [Stephen M. Schwebel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_M._Schwebel)

  [^cite_note-Schwebel-184]

  &#x20;made three distinctions specific to the Israeli situation to claim that the territories were seized in self-defense and that Israel has more title to them than the previous holders.

- [Julius Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Stone) also wrote that "Israel's presence in all these areas pending negotiation of new borders is entirely lawful, since Israel entered them lawfully in self-defense."

  [^cite_note-stone-185]

  &#x20;He argued that it would be an "irony bordering on the absurd" to read Article 49(6) as meaning that the State of Israel was obliged to ensure (by force if necessary) that areas with a millennial association with Jewish life shall be forever *"[judenrein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judenfrei)"*.

  [^cite_note-186]

Professor Ben Saul took exception to this view, arguing that Article 49(6) can be read to include voluntary or assisted transfers, as indeed it was in the advisory opinion of the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) which had expressed this interpretation in the [Israeli Wall Advisory Opinion (2003)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier).[^cite_note-187]

&#x20;Israel maintains that a temporary use of land and buildings for various purposes is permissible under a plea of military necessity and that the settlements fulfilled security needs.[^cite_note-ot-13] Israel argues that its settlement policy is consistent with international law, including the [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention), while recognising that some settlements have been constructed illegally on private land.[^cite_note-Harel-14] The Israeli Supreme Court has ruled that the power of the Civil Administration and the Military Commander in the occupied territories is limited by the entrenched customary rules of public international law as codified in the Hague Regulations.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190] In 1998 the Israeli Minister of Foreign Affairs produced "The International Criminal Court Background Paper".[^cite_note-191] It concludes

> International law has long recognised that there are crimes of such severity they should be considered "international crimes." Such crimes have been established in treaties such as the Genocide Convention and the Geneva Conventions... The following are Israel's primary issues of concern \[i.e. with the rules of the ICC]: The inclusion of settlement activity as a "war crime" is a cynical attempt to abuse the Court for political ends. The implication that the transfer of civilian population to occupied territories can be classified as a crime equal in gravity to attacks on civilian population centres or mass murder is preposterous and has no basis in international law.

A UN conference was held in Rome in 1998, where Israel was one of seven countries to vote against the Rome Statute to establish the [International Criminal Court](14880.md). Israel was opposed to a provision that included as a war crime the transfer of civilian populations into territory the government occupies.[^cite_note-192] Israel has signed the statute, but not ratified the treaty.[^cite_note-193]

## Land ownership



[Elon Moreh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elon_Moreh), West Bank

A 1996 amendment to an Israeli military order states that land privately owned can not be part of a settlement unless the land in question has been confiscated for military purposes.[^cite_note-Ofran,_Etkes-159] In 2006 [Peace Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_Now) acquired a report, which it claims was leaked from the Israeli Government's Civil Administration, indicating that up to 40 percent of the land Israel plans to retain in the West Bank is privately owned by Palestinians.[^cite_note-194] Peace Now called this a violation of Israeli law.[^cite_note-195] Peace Now published a comprehensive report about settlements on private lands.[^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197] In the wake of a legal battle, Peace Now lowered the figure to 32 percent, which the Civil Administration also denied.[^cite_note-198] *The Washington Post* reported that "The 38-page report offers what appears to be a comprehensive argument against the Israeli government's contention that it avoids building on private land, drawing on the state's own data to make the case."[^cite_note-199]

In February 2008, the Civil Administration stated that the land on which more than a third of West Bank settlements was built had been expropriated by the IDF for "security purposes."[^cite_note-200] The unauthorized seizure of private Palestinian land was defined by the Civil Administration itself as 'theft.'[^cite_note-201] According to [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem), more than 42 percent of the West Bank are under control of the Israeli settlements, 21 percent of which was seized from private Palestinian owners, much of it in violation of the 1979 Israeli Supreme Court decision.[^cite_note-CBSmain6650897-132]



[Neve Daniel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neve_Daniel), West Bank

In 1979, the government decided to extend settlements or build new ones only on "state lands".[^cite_note-Eldar_220711-89][^cite_note-Ofran,_Etkes-159]

A secret database, drafted by a retired senior officer, Baruch Spiegel, on orders from former [defense minister](3226446.md) [Shaul Mofaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaul_Mofaz), found that some settlements deemed legal by Israel were illegal outposts, and that large portions of [Ofra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ofra), [Elon Moreh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elon_Moreh) and [Beit El](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_El) were built on private Palestinian land. The "Spiegel report" was revealed by Haaretz in 2009. Many settlements are largely built on private lands, without approval of the Israeli Government.[^cite_note-Spiegel-202] According to Israel, the bulk of the land was vacant, was leased from the state, or bought fairly from Palestinian landowners.

Invoking the [Absentees' Property Laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Absentees'_Property_Laws) to transfer, sell or lease property in East Jerusalem owned by Palestinians who live elsewhere without compensation has been criticized both inside and outside of Israel.[^cite_note-203] Opponents of the settlements claim that "vacant" land belonged to Arabs who fled or collectively to an entire village, a practice that developed under [Ottoman](22278.md) rule. [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem) charged that Israel is using the absence of modern legal documents for the communal land as a legal basis for expropriating it. These "abandoned lands" are sometimes laundered through a series of fraudulent sales.[^cite_note-204]

According to Amira Hass, one of the techniques used by Israel to expropriate Palestinian land is to place desired areas under a "military firing zone" classification, and then issue orders for the evacuation of Palestinians from the villages in that range while allowing contiguous Jewish settlements to remain unaffected.[^cite_note-205]

## Effects on Palestinian human rights



Parts of the West Bank allocated to the settlements, as of January 2012 (in pink and purple color). Access is prohibited or restricted to Palestinians.

Amnesty International argues that Israel's settlement policy is discriminatory and a violation of Palestinian human rights.[^cite_note-Amnesty-206] B'Tselem claims that Israeli travel restrictions impact on [Palestinian freedom of movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_freedom_of_movement)[^cite_note-207] and Palestinian human rights have been violated in Hebron due to the presence of the settlers within the city.[^cite_note-Siege-208][^cite_note-Report-209][^cite_note-210] According to B'Tselem, over fifty percent of West Bank land expropriated from Palestinians has been used to establish settlements and create reserves of land for their future expansion. The seized lands mainly benefit the settlements and Palestinians cannot use them.[^cite_note-btselem.org-211] The roads built by Israel in the West Bank to serve the settlements are closed to Palestinian vehicles'[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213] and act as a barrier often between villages and the lands on which they subsist.[^cite_note-214]

Human Rights Watch and other human rights observer volunteer regularly file reports on "settler violence", referring to stoning and shooting incidents involving Israeli settlers.[^cite_note-215] Israel's withdrawal from Gaza and Hebron have led to violent settler protests and disputes over land and resources. [Meron Benvenisti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meron_Benvenisti) described the settlement enterprise as a "commercial real estate project that conscripts Zionist rhetoric for profit."[^cite_note-216]

The construction of the [Israeli West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier) has been criticized as an infringement on Palestinian human and land rights. The [United Nations Office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs](1602284.md) estimated that 10% of the West Bank would fall on the Israeli side of the barrier.[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218]

In July 2012, the [UN Human Rights Council](635790.md) decided to set up a probe into Jewish settlements. The report of [the independent international fact-finding mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_International_Fact-Finding_Mission_on_Israeli_Settlements) which investigated the "implications of the Israeli settlements on the civil, political, economic, social and cultural rights of the Palestinian people throughout the Occupied Palestinian Territory" was published in February 2013.[^cite_note-219]

In February 2020, the [Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights](22463.md) published a list of 112 companies linked to activities related to Israeli settlements in the occupied West Bank.[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221]

In a June 2026 report, Amnesty International accused the Israeli government of carrying out a campaign of [ethnic cleansing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing) against Palestinians in the occupied West Bank. According to the organization, the displacement of Palestinians was not simply the result of violence by individual settlers but formed part of a broader state policy. Amnesty also argued that settler violence played a significant role in the process. Citing [United Nations](31769.md) data, the report said that more than 100 Palestinian communities had been fully or partly emptied between 2023 and 2026, while thousands of other Palestinians were displaced following demolitions carried out by Israeli authorities. The report examined 27 villages and hamlets where Palestinians were displaced between 2023 and 2025 and said that settlement growth and the creation of new outposts had increased since October 2023.[^cite_note-222]

## Economy



Many residents of [Ma'ale Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ale_Adumim) work in [Mishor Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mishor_Adumim) industrial park

Goods produced in Israeli settlements are able to stay competitive on the global market, in part because of massive state subsidies they receive from the Israeli government. Farmers and producers are given state assistance, while companies that set up in the territories receive tax breaks and direct government subsidies. An Israeli government fund has also been established to help companies pay customs penalties.[^cite_note-SpiegelSC-223] Palestinian officials estimate that settlers sell goods worth some \$500 million to the Palestinian market.[^cite_note-Reuters-224] Israel has built 16 industrial zones, containing roughly 1000 industrial plants, in the West Bank and East Jerusalem on acreage that consumes large parts of the territory planned for a future Palestinian state. According to Jodi Rudoren these installations both entrench the occupation and provide work for Palestinians, even those opposed to it. The 16 parks are located at [Shaked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaked), [Beka'ot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beka'ot), [Baran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baran,_Israel?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Karnei Shomron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karnei_Shomron), [Emmanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immanuel_\(town\)), [Barkan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barkan), [Ariel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_\(city\)), [Shilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shilo,_Mateh_Binyamin), [Halamish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halamish), [Ma'ale Efraim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ale_Efraim), [Sha'ar Binyamin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sha'ar_Binyamin_Industrial_Zone), [Atarot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atarot), [Mishor Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mishor_Adumim), [Gush Etzion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Etzion), [Kiryat Arba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Arba) and [Metarim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metarim?action=edit\&redlink=1) (2001).[^cite_note-225] In spite of this, the West Bank settlements have failed to develop a self-sustaining local economy. About 60% of the settler workforce commutes to Israel for work. The settlements rely primarily on the labor of their residents in Israel proper rather than local manufacturing, agriculture, or research and development. Of the industrial parks in the settlements, there are only two significant ones, at Ma'ale Adumim and Barkan, with most of the workers there being Palestinian. Only a few hundred settler households cultivate agricultural land, and rely primarily on Palestinian labor in doing so.[^cite_note-226]

Settlement has an economic dimension, much of it driven by the significantly lower costs of housing for Israeli citizens living in Israeli settlements compared to the cost of housing and living in Israel proper.[^cite_note-maannews.net-60] Government spending per citizen in the settlements is double that spent per Israeli citizen in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and [Jerusalem](16043.md), while government spending for settlers in isolated Israeli settlements is three times the Israeli national average. Most of the spending goes to the security of the Israeli citizens living there.[^cite_note-Rudoren-61]

### Export to EU

According to Israeli government estimates, \$230 million worth of settler goods including fruit, vegetables, cosmetics, textiles and toys are exported to the EU each year, accounting for approximately 2% of all Israeli exports to Europe.[^cite_note-SpiegelSC-223] A 2013 report of Profundo revealed that at least 38 Dutch companies imported settlement products.[^cite_note-Profundo-227]

[European Union](9317.md) law requires a distinction to be made between goods originating in Israel and those from the occupied territories. The former benefit from preferential custom treatment according to the [EU-Israel Association Agreement (2000)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93European_Union_relations); the latter do not, having been explicitly excluded from the agreement.[^cite_note-SpiegelSC-223][^cite_note-g20091210-228] In practice, however, settler goods often avoid mandatory customs through being labelled as originating in Israel, while European customs authorities commonly fail to complete obligatory postal code checks of products to ensure they have not originated in the occupied territories.[^cite_note-SpiegelSC-223][^cite_note-Profundo-227]

In 2009, the [United Kingdom](31717.md)'s Department for the Environment, Food and Rural Affairs issued new guidelines concerning labelling of goods imported from the West Bank. The new guidelines require labelling to clarify whether West Bank products originate from settlements or from the Palestinian economy. Israel's foreign ministry said that the UK was "catering to the demands of those whose ultimate goal is the boycott of Israeli products"; but this was denied by the UK government, who said that the aim of the new regulations was to allow consumers to choose for themselves what produce they buy.[^cite_note-g20091210-228] Denmark has similar legislation requiring food products from settlements in the occupied territories to be accurately labelled.[^cite_note-SpiegelSC-223] In June 2022, Norway also stated that it would begin complying with EU regulation to label produce originating from Israeli settlements in the West Bank and Golan Heights as such.[^cite_note-229]

On 12 November 2019 the Court of Justice of the European Union in a ruling[^cite_note-230] covering all territory Israel captured in the 1967 war decided that labels on foodstuffs must not imply that goods produced in occupied territory came from Israel itself and must "prevent consumers from being misled as to the fact that the State of Israel is present in the territories concerned as an occupying power and not as a sovereign entity". In its ruling, the court said that failing to inform EU consumers they were potentially buying goods produced in settlements denies them access to "ethical considerations and considerations relating to the observance of international law".[^cite_note-231]

In January 2019 the [Dáil Éireann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%A1il_%C3%89ireann) (Ireland's [lower house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_house)) voted in favour, by 78 to 45, of the [Control of Economic Activity (Occupied Territories) Bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupied_Territories_Bill).[^cite_note-232] This piece of legislation prohibits the purchasing of any good and/or service from the [Golan Heights](7515865.md), [East Jerusalem](343508.md) or [West Bank](33209.md) settlements. The Bill made no further progress until 2024 when the then government sought legal advice from the [Attorney General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney-General_for_Ireland) in response to the [International Court of Justice](14918.md)'s advisory opinion on [Israel's occupation of the Palestinian territories](72240449.md).[^cite_note-233] Following the Attorney General's advice the [Tánaiste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A1naiste) and [Minister for Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Foreign_Affairs_and_Trade), [Micheál Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miche%C3%A1l_Martin) confirmed on 22 October 2024 that the Bill would be "reviewed and amendments prepared in order to bring in into line with the Constitution and EU Law".[^cite_note-234] On 31 October 2024, it was reported that a technical blockage of the Bill would be removed to allow it to proceed to committee stage, however the Bill was not passed before the Dáil was suspended [*sine die*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adjournment_sine_die) on the 7 November 2024 marking the end of the [33rd Dáil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/33rd_D%C3%A1il).

A petition under the [European Citizens' Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Citizens'_Initiative), submitted in September 2021, was accepted on 20 February 2022. The petition seeks the adoption of legislation to ban trade with unlawful settlements. The petition requires a million signatures from across the EU and has received support from civil society groups including [Human Rights Watch](46530.md).[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239]

In August 2026, the Dutch government banned products from Israeli settlements in the West Bank, East Jerusalem and the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-t912-240]

### Palestinian economy and resources

A Palestinian report argued in 2011 that settlements have a detrimental effect on the Palestinian economy, equivalent to about 85% of the nominal gross domestic product of Palestine, and that the "occupation enterprise" allows the state of Israel and commercial firms to profit from Palestinian natural resources and tourist potential.[^cite_note-g20110929-241] A 2013 report published by the [World Bank](36752.md) analysed the impact that the limited access to Area C lands and resources had on the Palestinian economy. While settlements represent a single axis of control, it is the largest with 68% of the Area C lands reserved for the settlements. The report goes on to calculate that access to the lands and resources of Area C, including the territory in and around settlements, would increase the Palestinian GDP by some \$3.5 billion (or 35%) per year.[^cite_note-242]

The Israeli Supreme Court has ruled that Israeli companies are entitled to exploit the West Bank's natural resources for economic gain, and that international law must be "adapted" to the "reality on the ground" of long-term occupation.[^cite_note-g20110103-243]

## Palestinian labour

Due to the availability of jobs offering twice the prevailing salary of the West Bank (as of August 2013), as well as high unemployment, tens of thousands of Palestinians work in Israeli settlements.[^cite_note-am20140218-244][^cite_note-jpost.com-245] According to the [Manufacturers Association of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturers_Association_of_Israel), some 22,000 Palestinians were employed in construction, agriculture, manufacturing and service industries.[^cite_note-246] An [Al-Quds University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quds_University) study in 2011 found that 82% of Palestinian workers said they would prefer to not work in Israeli settlements if they had alternative employment in the West Bank.[^cite_note-am20140218-244]

Palestinians have been highly involved in the construction of settlements in the West Bank. In 2013, the Palestinian Central Bureau of Statistics released their survey showing that the number of Palestinian workers who are employed by the Jewish settlements increased from 16,000 to 20,000 in the first quarter.[^cite_note-jpost.com-245] The survey also found that Palestinians who work in Israel and the settlements are paid more than twice their salary compared to what they receive from Palestinian employers.[^cite_note-jpost.com-245]

In 2008, [Kav LaOved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kav_LaOved) charged that Palestinians who work in Israeli settlements are not granted basic protections of Israeli labor law. Instead, they are employed under [Jordanian](7515964.md) labor law, which does not require minimum wage, payment for overtime and other social rights. In 2007, the [Supreme Court of Israel](1666510.md) ruled that Israeli labor law does apply to Palestinians working in West Bank settlements and applying different rules in the same work place constituted discrimination. The ruling allowed Palestinian workers to file lawsuits in Israeli courts. In 2008, the average sum claimed by such lawsuits stood at 100,000 shekels.[^cite_note-247]

According to the Palestinian Center for Policy and Survey Research, 63% of Palestinians opposed PA plans to prosecute Palestinians who work in the settlements. However, 72% of Palestinians support a boycott of the products they sell.[^cite_note-248] Although the Palestinian Authority has criminalized working in the settlements, the director-general at the Palestinian Ministry of Labor, Samer Salameh, described the situation in February 2014 as being "caught between two fires". He said "We strongly discourage work in the settlements, since the entire enterprise is illegal and illegitimate...but given the high unemployment rate and the lack of alternatives, we do not enforce the law that criminalizes work in the settlements."[^cite_note-am20140218-244]

## Violence

### Israeli settler violence

Main article: [Israeli settler violence](47246246.md)

Further information: [Hilltop Youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilltop_Youth)



Graffiti in [Hebron](38577.md), in the [Israeli-occupied](2781576.md) West Bank, calling for [the gassing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gas_chamber) of Arabs, above a [tag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tag_\(graffiti\)) for the right-wing group the [Jewish Defense League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Defense_League)

[^cite_note-249]

[^cite_note-250]



Olive trees in the village of Burin allegedly vandalized by settlers from the settlement [Yitzhar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhar) in November 2009

[Gush Emunim Underground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Emunim_Underground) was a militant organization that operated in 1979–1984. The organization planned attacks on Palestinian officials and the [Dome of the Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dome_of_the_Rock).[^cite_note-nytimes1-251][^cite_note-jta1-252] In 1994, [Baruch Goldstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baruch_Goldstein) of Hebron, a member of [Kach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kach_and_Kahane_Chai) carried out the [Cave of the Patriarchs massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cave_of_the_Patriarchs_massacre), killing 29 Muslim worshipers and injuring 125. The attack was widely condemned by the Israeli government and Jewish community. The Palestinian leadership has accused Israel of "encouraging and enabling" settler violence in a bid to provoke Palestinian riots and violence in retaliation.[^cite_note-253] Violence perpetrated by Israeli settlers against Palestinians constitutes terrorism according to the U.S. Department of State, and former IDF Head of Central Command Avi Mizrahi stated that such violence constitutes "terror."[^cite_note-254]

In mid-2008, a UN report recorded 222 acts of [Israeli settler violence](47246246.md) against Palestinians and IDF troops compared with 291 in 2007.[^cite_note-bbc1-255] This trend reportedly increased in 2009.[^cite_note-HaaretzViolentSettlers-256] Maj-Gen Shamni said that the number had risen from a few dozen individuals to hundreds, and called it "a very grave phenomenon."[^cite_note-bbc1-255] In 2008–2009, the defense establishment adopted a harder line against the extremists.[^cite_note-HaaretzViolentSettlers-256] This group responded with a tactic dubbed "[price tagging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Price_tag_attack_policy)", vandalizing Palestinian property whenever police or soldiers were sent in to dismantle outposts.[^cite_note-Price_Tag-257] From January through to September 2013, 276 attacks by settlers against Palestinians were recorded.[^cite_note-258]

Leading religious figures in the West Bank have harshly criticized these tactics. Rabbi [Menachem Froman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Froman) of [Tekoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tekoa,_Gush_Etzion) said that "Targeting Palestinians and their property is a shocking thing, ... It's an act of hurting humanity. ... This builds a wall of fire between Jews and Arabs."[^cite_note-ynetnews.com-259] The Yesha Council and [Hanan Porat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanan_Porat) also condemned such actions.[^cite_note-260] Other rabbis have been accused of inciting violence against non-Jews.[^cite_note-261] In response to settler violence, the Israeli government said that it would increase law enforcement and cut off aid to illegal outposts.[^cite_note-262] Some settlers are thought to lash out at Palestinians because they are "easy victims."[^cite_note-263] The United Nations accused Israel of failing to intervene and arrest settlers suspected of violence.[^cite_note-264] In 2008, Haaretz wrote that "Israeli society has become accustomed to seeing lawbreaking settlers receive special treatment and no other group could similarly attack Israeli law enforcement agencies without being severely punished."[^cite_note-haaretz2-265]

In September 2011, settlers vandalized a mosque and an army base.
